> **US-059 fix pass (PC-B programmer, 2026-09-25) -> `po-review`:** all 5 PO-REJECT fixes done, same 2 files only. (1) Real GPU by default: new `buildLaunchFlags(opts, platform)` returns `['--use-angle=d3d11']` on `win32` and `[]` (Chrome's own default) elsewhere; the old unconditional `--use-gl=angle --use-angle=swiftshader --enable-unsafe-swiftshader --ignore-gpu-blocklist` only comes back behind a new opt-in `--swiftshader` CLI flag. (2) Fail-fast: `Runtime.enable` + a `Runtime.consoleAPICalled` listener catches the page's own `[webgl2Gate] software renderer detected` warning (new `isSoftwareRendererLine()`); in `gpucompare`/`voxelbench` mode `waitForGlobal` now checks that line (and, as a second signal, `window.__debug.rt.backend !== 'gl2'`) on every poll and throws immediately instead of waiting out `--timeout-ms`. (3) Profile dir moved from the repo root to `path.join(os.tmpdir(), 'kestrel-capture-browser-profile-<port>')`; `cleanup()` (called on normal exit, on error via `finally`, and on `SIGINT`/`SIGTERM`) now `rmSync`s it. (4) `validatePcbPort` -> `validatePort`, range widened to 9000-9999 (still always rejects 8000), error message names both "9000-9499 (PC-A)" and "9500-9999 (PC-B)"; `validatePcbPort` kept as an exported alias in case anything else still imports the old name. (5) `tools/capture-browser.test.mjs`: added checks for `buildLaunchFlags` (win32 default = d3d11 no swiftshader flags, non-win32 default = no flags, `--swiftshader` on either platform = swiftshader flags and never d3d11 alongside them), `parseArgs` picking up `--swiftshader`, `isSoftwareRendererLine` matching the real webgl2Gate.js text and ignoring unrelated console lines, and widened `validatePort` range checks (9000/9499/9500/9999 accepted, 8999/8000/10000/non-integer rejected). **D-009 (2026-09-23):** the renderer moves to a staged full-GPU per-cell pipeline. US-029 is a hard gate: if it fails `?gpucompare=1` or does not run on the owner's hardware, and one rework does not fix it, M1 falls back to plan A (US-004c, CPU optimisations, to be written then), US-030 is dropped from M1 and US-006/007/016 are built on the CPU. The JS path stays the oracle and the fallback in every case.

**D-011 (2026-09-23, amendments 1+2, D-013):** new story canon (game title *Kestrel*; hero Wick from machine-only Ferrum, no amnesia; magic real outside). Geometry, beats, numbers and engine order are unchanged; US-011/012/013/014/016/017/021/022 are text/art reskins, and US-015 is the only scope change (map card + `M`). Each story has a "D-011 reskin" block. Approved old art stays valid for format and geometry; the PO re-checks the reskinned art. The Settings menu (US-038) is **M2 P1** per D-012: M1 ships the 240x90 default plus `?grid=WxH`. If the US-030b owner walk-test finds 240x90 unreadable on common screens, the PO pulls a grid-only slice of US-038 into M1 as P0.

M1 exit criteria = all P0 stories `done` (roadmap), and `node tools/check-deps.mjs` reports no engine imports from `game/` or `design/`. US-022 (P1) and P2 stories are not exit criteria.

### US-070b Terrain shows structure/prop sun shadows  [Priority: P1 (M2)] [Status: todo]  [PC-A]

After US-070a.

## Mesh engine phases 0-1 (D-029, architecture.md 27; sprint 4)
**Rules for every ME story below (27.11, 27.13):** architect addendum in the row before start; engine stories end in `arch-review` then PO; every new engine file starts with `// @ts-check` and carries full JSDoc (`@param`/`@returns` on every export, `@type` on typed-array fields, no `any` in exported signatures, no `.d.ts`, no TypeScript source) per ME-00; `node tools/run-tests.mjs` + `node tools/check-deps.mjs` green (typecheck suite may WARN on legacy files, must be clean on the story's own files); no per-frame allocation (`--expose-gc` probe where 27.7 item 5 lists the module); no `gl.*` outside `engine/render/gpu/device/` from ME-03b on; no three.js/gl-matrix/runtime deps; nothing deleted before ME-19; `dda/terrain/voxel.frag` untouched (frozen). Phase-4-safe GLSL (27.11): `texelFetch` only, typed-array uniform blocks, no `sampler2DShadow`, no `EXT_*` beyond the timer.

### Mesh phase-1 gate  [Status: PASSED (owner GO 2026-09-29, D-029 amendment 1)] - gate data table moved to `docs/backlog-archive.md` (pass d).

### ME-12 Phase-2 browser pass + gate report on `?renderer=mesh&physics=mesh`  [Priority: P1 (sprint 5)] [Status: todo]  [PC-A]
As the owner, I want to play the whole M1 route on mesh rendering AND mesh collision and see that it plays the same, so that I can give the phase-2 go/no-go (27.11).
Acceptance criteria:
- [ ] 1. Route on `?renderer=mesh&physics=mesh`, played start to end without a stuck or fall-through: wake -> stairs -> lever/grate -> boulder -> breach -> hillside -> waystone. Each leg checked by a headless scripted walk (`tools/capture-browser.mjs` or a bench walk) and once by hand.
- [ ] 2. Parity with grid physics (US-008/009 behaviour): step-up on stair edges, no snag at doorways, lintel blocks head height, gaps not walkable, jump arc and landing heights within 0.05 cells of `physics=grid` on the same inputs, terrain slopes walk/slide as US-026a. Any remaining difference is listed and explained in the gate table.
- [ ] 3. Roller/boulder rolls, stops and hits walls as before (same stop cell +-0.25 on the same push); grate blocks while closed, opens on the lever, and the player/boulder passes through the open grate (dynamic collider follows the sector anim, no gap or ghost wall).
- [ ] 4. Save round trip: save mid-route (grate open, boulder moved), reload on `physics=mesh`, world and colliders identical (per architecture.md 27.18: `serialize` round trip + 200 seeded `collideCircle`/`supportAt` probes bit-equal; no separate state hash needed), and the walk continues.
- [ ] 5. Replay determinism: the 600-step replay test (ME-11c) gives bit-equal body state on two runs and on `physics=grid` vs `mesh` only where 2 says they may differ; test green in run-tests.
- [ ] 6. Sim time: F3 sim p95 <= 1 ms and `?bench=1` sim <= 1 ms on the walk at 400x150 (also record 240x90); zero per-frame allocation in the collide path (existing alloc probe).
- [ ] 7. Frame budget: GPU p95 <= the phase-1 numbers (2.52 ms at 400x150, 1.34 ms at 240x90) + 0.1 ms; JS <= 8 ms; `over25 == 0` on the walk.
- [ ] 8. All Node suites (`node tools/run-tests.mjs`), `node tools/check-deps.mjs`, typecheck green; `?gpucompare=1` all poses PASS (34/34 incl. forestEdge) on the owner GPU in both renderers.
- [ ] 9. Fixes found in the browser pass are made in this story (or filed as BUG rows with an owner-agreed cut); at most one fix round before the gate.
- [ ] 10. Phase-2 gate report table in this section, same style as the phase-1 gate (`### Mesh phase-1 gate`): rows = route legs 1-7 above + suites + gpucompare + bench; columns = criterion, target, grid, mesh, result, note. Architect verdict recorded.
- [ ] 11. Owner walk-test ("plays the same": no new snags on stairs/doorways/lintels, grate, boulder, terrain walk-out) and owner GO/NO-GO recorded as an amendment to D-029 in `docs/decisions.md` (ESCALATE TO MANAGER for the write-up). No-go after one fix round -> manager decision (fallback in 27.11).
Design needed: no.
Notes / dependencies: 27.11 phase 2. Deps ME-11 (arch-reviewed), ME-08. GO unlocks ME-12b, ME-19, ME-21, M3 stories (D-030 item 1).

| BUG-CLOTH-001 | **`sectorCaster.invariance.test.js` (CO-3, large-world-offset invariance) fails once a cloth is actually drawn near a test camera pose** (found 2026-10-03, PC-B programmer agent, CLOTH-1b5: placing `tower.stairwell.canvas` makes the 'inside, facing lantern/brazier' pose draw it for the first time; confirmed 100% reproducible - 6/7 fails with the cloth in place, 7/7 passes on the pre-change content, re-checked directly against `git show HEAD^:content/levels/tower.level.json`). Root cause traced to `engine/mesh/DrawList.js` `pushClothItem` (~line 329): it rounds the cloth's **absolute world-space** bbox-center to the nearest metre (`Math.round` on float64) to build the mesh's local origin, then stores `mesh.pos` world-minus-that-origin in a **Float32Array**. CO-3 compares the same scene placed at world origin A vs a large offset B/B'; the float64 bbox-center lands near an exact `.5` rounding boundary in A and a slightly different one after the large-offset addition in B, and that tiny residual survives into the Float32Array position as a real (if tiny, ~2.4e-7) bit difference in one pixel's depth (`3.7775514125823975` vs `3.7775511741638184`). Latent since CLOTH-1b2/1b3 (their own fixtures never placed a cloth inside a structure far from world origin while a CO-3-style camera pose drew it) - CLOTH-1b5 is just the first real content to trigger it, not a regression it introduced | P2 (cosmetic-scale float precision; CO-3 is a bit-identical parity gate, not a visible-artifact one) | todo - `NEEDS PC-A: ASK ARCHITECT` - decide the fix (candidates, not evaluated: round in the cloth's structure-local frame before converting to world so the rounding boundary moves with the structure, not the camera-relative residual; or carry the cloth mesh origin as a Float64 and only narrow to Float32 for the GPU upload, matching how terrain/voxel meshes avoid this; or widen CO-3's tolerance specifically for the cloth draw path). Currently left failing on `pc-b` (3rd suite FAIL alongside the 2 pre-existing flakes) until the architect decides | [PC-A] `engine/mesh/DrawList.js` - repro: `node engine/render/sectorCaster.invariance.test.js` on `pc-b` HEAD. **Architect decision 2026-10-03:** cause = cloth sim + 60-step warm-up run in absolute f64 world coords, not bit-identical under translation (~1e-13 m survives the f32 narrowing as 1 ulp; not `Math.round`). Fix (PC-B, ~15 min): `engine/render/sectorCaster.invariance.test.js:63` `mk()` -> after `World.load` set `w.cloths = createClothSystem([], null, null)` (CO-3 tests static structures; cloth excluded by rule). No DrawList change. Architect adds "cloth is not bit-translation-invariant (accepted)" to 33.5. -> Q13 item 1. **done 2026-10-03 (PC-B main session):** exactly the prescribed fix - `createClothSystem` imported, `mk()` sets `w.cloths = createClothSystem([], null, null)` right after `World.load`. `sectorCaster.invariance.test.js` 7/7 PASS; `run-tests.mjs` back to 192 PASS/2 FAIL (the 2 pre-existing unrelated flakes only)/1 WARN; `check-deps.mjs` clean. |
| BUG-VM-001 | **The held sword view-model (US-078a `engine.viewModel`) never draws in normal play - only its trail line is visible** (confirmed 2026-10-02, PC-B main session, matches the owner's walk-test report "it's just a line"). Root cause, isolated precisely: `engine/render/viewModel.js` `buildList(cam, pitched)` (line ~247) returns `null` (nothing drawn) whenever `pitched || cam.projection === 'pitched'`; `GpuCellPipeline.js` passes its own `this._pitched = resolveProjection(cam, this.renderer) === 'pitched'` as that `pitched` arg (line 1770/1634); `engine/render/projection.js` `resolveProjection(cam, renderer)` returns `'pitched'` for `renderer === 'mesh'` whenever `cam.projection` isn't explicitly `'shear'`. `game/js/main.js`'s own `cam` object never sets `.projection` at all, and `effRenderer` is `'mesh'` by default (RE-02b/D-029) - so on the shipped default config, `this._pitched` is **always true**, and the view model's draw list is **always null**. `swordView.js`'s `drawTrail` calls `vm.mountEye(...)` independently of `vm.show()`'s success (it just samples the clip's static pose data), which is why the trail still drew even though the sword model itself never did - exactly the reported symptom. **Why US-078a's own gpucompare poses (53/53 mesh) didn't catch this:** those poses are parity checks (JS twin vs GPU twin agree), and since the pose is also mesh-rendered, `buildList` returns `null` on BOTH twins - "both sides draw nothing" is a passing parity result, not a real-visibility check. Likely cause: the "pitched = layer off" rule in architecture.md 30.1 was written/accepted before (or without connecting to) 28.1 Amendment 2 / RE-02b making `'pitched'` the mesh renderer's own default first-person projection (previously "pitched" meant only the old RTS top-down camera) - the rule now disables the feature under its own primary target config. Not something PC-B should patch solo: this is engine/render code under architect review | P0 (US-078d is otherwise feel-complete but invisible) | todo - `NEEDS PC-A: ASK ARCHITECT` - decide the real fix (candidates, not evaluated here: drop the `pitched` gate entirely now that RE-02b/mesh-pitched is the normal first-person case and let the view model project through the pitched camera math same as everything else in the pitched pipeline; or keep a narrower gate for whatever the ORIGINAL "pitched" case was meant to exclude, if that's still distinct from today's mesh-pitched default) | [PC-A] `engine/render/viewModel.js`, `engine/render/gpu/GpuCellPipeline.js`, `engine/render/projection.js` - repro: load `game/index.html` (default params, no `?renderer=` override), pick up the sword, confirm `engine.viewModel.buildList(cam, true)` (pitched=true, matching the real runtime call) returns `null` even right after a successful `vm.show(...)`. **Architect decision 2026-10-03:** cause = the 30.1 rule "pitched = layer off" (`engine/render/viewModel.js:247`) predates RE-02b, so the default mesh first-person frame always returns null; also `_eyeMap` (~l.225) is a shear map (`Aw[7] = -tp`), wrong for a rotating pitched camera. Fix (~0.5 d, engine -> opus re-review): (1) viewModel.js:247 drop `pitched || cam.projection === 'pitched'` from the gate (the game hides the layer where there is no hand); (2) `_eyeMap(cam, Aw, pitched)` pitched = true rotation `[c, -s*cp, -s*sp; s, c*cp, c*sp; 0, -sp, cp]`, latch `this._pitched` in `buildList` so `eyeToWorld` + `mountEye` (trail) use the same map; (3) viewModel.test.js:122-123 invert to "pitched -> list non-null" + test rotation map == shear map within 1e-3 at pitch 0 and +-5 deg; (4) gpucompare visibility pose: sword shown, mesh pitched, view-model cells > 0 on BOTH twins; (5) owner walk-test: sword in hand + swing. -> Q13 item 5 (PC-B cross-track). **Done 2026-10-03 (PC-B programmer agent, main session finished + verified after the agent was stopped mid-task):** (1) `viewModel.js:247` gate now only checks `this._h < 0` (no model bound) - the `pitched`/`cam.projection` checks are gone. (2) `_eyeMap(cam, Aw, pitched)` gains a true-rotation branch for `pitched` (unchanged shear math when false); `buildList` latches `this._pitched` once per call, `eyeToWorld` reads it so the trail (`swordView.js`'s `drawTrail` via `mountEye`+`eyeToWorld`) and the model agree every frame. (3) `viewModel.test.js`: the old "pitched -> null" tests inverted to "pitched -> non-null with items"; a new rotation-vs-shear convergence block found the architect's literal "within 1e-3 at +-5 deg" ask is **not achievable by construction** (the two conventions share only a 0th-order term at pitch=0 - at +-5deg the true rotation has a first-order `Aw[5] = c*sin(pitch)` term the shear map structurally lacks, ~0.09 gap, not 1e-3) - implemented instead: exact equality at pitch=0 (1e-9), a documented ~0.09 bound at +-5deg, and genuine 1e-3 convergence at a smaller +-0.05deg - flagging the literal tolerance as an architect re-review item, not silently changed. (4) gpucompare: 2 new `PITCHED CAMERA` poses (pitch 0, 20) with `vmAssert: true` - captures `engine.viewModel.stats.items` separately right after each twin's own render call (GPU twin's real work happens inside `rt.present()`, not `gpuPipeline.frame()`) and asserts `> 0` on both, not just that the twins agree (the exact vacuousness that let this bug ship past 53/53 prior poses). (5) owner walk-test not done live (no interactive browser here) - a headless pickup simulation (teleport to the sword, synthetic `KeyE`) did not register the interaction (likely a facing/cone nuance in `engine/world/interaction.js`'s target-finding, not re-diagnosed - zero-distance teleport may be a degenerate case for its direction check), so that specific script is inconclusive; the real regression proof is the gpucompare `vmAssert` poses instead (below), which directly show `engine.viewModel.buildList` populated under the pitched camera with a bound model on both twins - stronger evidence than a scripted pickup would add. **Re-verified independently by the main session before push:** reverted one unexplained, uncommented change the killed agent left in `game/js/dev/modes/gpucompare.js` (the 2 pre-existing shear poses' pitch array `[0,30]` had been changed to `[0,20]` with no comment and a stale doc-comment still saying "+30" - restored to `[0,30]`, original baseline untouched). `node engine/render/viewModel.test.js` 33/33; `node tools/run-tests.mjs` 193 PASS/2 FAIL (pre-existing)/1 WARN; `check-deps.mjs` OK. Real-GPU `node tools/capture-browser.mjs --mode gpucompare --query "gpucompare=1&renderer=mesh&grid=160x60"`: all 4 pre-existing shear view-model poses PASS (pitch 0/30 x rest/swingLR, unaffected by this fix as designed); new PITCHED pitch=0 pose PASSes outright; new PITCHED pitch=20 pose **FAILs on overall geometry/cell parity** (`cmpCells.pass`/`cmpGeom.pass` false - a handful of cells, `geomViolCells: 4`, a `nrmMaxDeg` ~90.001deg normal-flip edge case already present even at pitch 0, plus one extra `faceViol`/`edgeKindMismatch` at pitch 20) **but `vmAssert`'s own check PASSes clean at both pitches** (`vmItemsGpu: 1, vmItemsJs: 1, vmOk: true`) - i.e. the actual bug (sword never drawing) is proven fixed on real GPU hardware; the pitch=20 pose's residual JS/GPU pixel-parity gap is a separate, much smaller cosmetic-scale issue (first real content to exercise the mesh renderer's pitched camera + view model together) - `NEEDS PC-A: ASK ARCHITECT` whether to tighten the fix further or accept/tune the pose's tolerance, same pattern as the BUG-CLOTH-001/BUG-ME-13 type findings elsewhere in this backlog. -> `arch-review` (opus). **fc2b401 ARCH CHANGES (small, opus 2026-10-03):** rotation map correct; gate removal + `_pitched` latch right; the "1e-3 at +-5 deg" bar was wrong (maps differ at first order). Required: (1) `viewModel.test.js` ~l.160 replace the "<0.09 ballpark" check with (a) pitched map orthonormal, det +1 within 1e-9, (b) screen-lock: tip via `eyeToWorld` (pitched) projected with `pitchedTerms(...).M` gives the same column/row within 0.5 cell at pitch 0 and +-20 deg; (2) `viewModel.js` l.12-14 header: one line for the pitched branch; (3) pitch-20 pose 4 FAIL cells: run `?gpucompare=1&renderer=mesh&pose=PITCHED` with the view model hidden - if the 4 cells stay, file a new BUG and move the pose to yaw 35; if they vanish, it is a view-model parity bug (fix). -> PC-B QUEUE 14 item 1. **Owner walk-test 2026-10-03 evening: "sword looks good, but should be in the left hand"** -> mirror the held-sword mount to the left of the view (mount x sign + swing arc mirrored, trail too); fold into Q14 item 1. |
| BUG-TUNNEL-001 | **Rare fall-through-the-floor on `physics=mesh` right as the tower grate opens** (found 2026-10-02, PC-B main session, `tools/route-walk-browser.mjs` post-merge re-check: 1 failure in 8 runs, port 9605, leg "5b grate open + upper steps"). Player's z drops from ~0.37 to -279 over the following 3 legs (never regains ground; `endTriggered: true` still fires since that trigger is an xy-area check) while `fell: true`/`minGap` goes deeply negative. The one failing run's `perf.worstIntervalMs` was far above the normal 18-28 ms range the other 7 clean runs show (a severe real-time frame stall, not a step-count/content issue - `simMax`/`jsMax` stayed in the usual few-ms range). Suspect: `engine/core/loop.js`'s `MAX_STEPS_PER_FRAME = 5` catch-up cap drops the remaining accumulator backlog outright on a bad stall ("prevents runaway update bursts" - intentional, documented) instead of ever finishing those sim steps; if that stall lands while `animateSector('grate', ...)` is mid-tween, something about the dropped-vs-resumed state around `refitDynCollider`'s barrier quads (27.18b, `engine/world/colliders.js`) may let the capsule's next `resolveBodyContacts` check miss the (now different) collider state for one step and never find ground again under the tower. Unconfirmed - this is an observation + a hypothesis, not a diagnosed fix. Not reproduced in 7 follow-up runs (same machine, same script). **Reproduced again 2026-10-03 (PC-B, port 9630, same leg) - this time with `perf.worstIntervalMs` in the NORMAL range (19.1 ms, `over25: 0`)**, weakening the "only under a severe stall" theory above: 5 further runs that session were clean, including one with a 264 ms stall that did NOT trigger it. Updated read: still rare (~2 in ~20 runs across both sessions) and still correlates with this exact leg (right as the grate opens), but the trigger condition is not simply "stall severity" - may be a narrower timing window (e.g. a specific accumulator/tick-count value at the exact moment the grate's `animateSector` tween crosses a boundary) that a stall only happens to land on sometimes. **Reproduced a 3rd time 2026-10-03 (port 9650, same leg; immediate retry on port 9651 was clean)** - frequency across both sessions now ~3 in ~22 runs. Confirmed unrelated to that session's own changes (particle-system wiring only, no physics/collision touch) by the immediate clean retry. | P2 (rare; still unresolved; the sandbox's own frame-hitch severity seems to gate it some of the time, not reliably) | todo - `NEEDS PC-A: architect note` (engine/core/loop.js catch-up policy + engine/world/colliders.js dynamic-collider refit interaction; decide whether a bad stall should ever leave the capsule able to end a step inside/below a dynamic collider, e.g. a conservative "re-check ground after any dropped-backlog frame" rule, vs accepting this as a sandbox-only artifact that real players essentially never hit) | [PC-A] `engine/core/loop.js`, `engine/world/colliders.js` - repro: `node tools/route-walk-browser.mjs --port <9500-9999>` repeatedly on `?renderer=mesh`; look for a leg with `fell: true` and a hugely negative `minGap`/`perf.worstIntervalMs` far above ~30 ms. |

| CLOTH-1 | **Cloth physics - "nice clothes"** (owner 2026-10-01: "physics of clothes ... I want nice clothes"). Owner quality bar: cloth must look and move well, not a stiff placeholder. Uses: torn balloon canvas in the tower stairwell swaying in the breach wind, banners/flags on ruins, wreck ropes, later NPC capes/robes (M3 exiles). Sketch: `engine/physics/cloth.js` (stand-alone rule), Verlet mass-spring grid with structural + shear + bend constraints, pins, gravity + shared gusting wind field (also feeds the US-020b wind sound), collision with ground/capsules/boxes, optional tearing for the balloon canvas; fixed step + deterministic (replay-safe); rendered as a per-frame mesh on the mesh renderer (two-sided, smooth normals so light/shadow read across folds), ASCII canvas glyphs `~ ) (` from the designer; budget a dozen cloths < 0.1-0.2 ms CPU. ### RTS-01 RTS readability spike (gate)  [Priority: P0] [Status: todo]  [PC-A]
As the owner, I want to walk around a close StarCraft-style view of ~200 ASCII units on the hillside and move groups of them, so that I can decide whether the RTS genre works in this look before we plan it (D-032).
**Setup (both steps):** dev page `game/rts-test.html` (own bootstrap, reuses the main.js AssetRegistry builder via import or a <= 5-line `main.js` export; `?renderer=mesh` forced, `renderer:'dda'` not offered), world = existing hillside `world_m1` (tower stays as terrain landmark), spike code in `game/js/rts/` (sim state SoA; `game/js/rts/sim/` for the tick code). Grid selectable 400x150 (default) / 240x90.
Acceptance criteria - **RTS-01a** (camera, units, selection):
- [ ] a1. Camera `cam.projection:'pitched'` via RE-03 `updateRtsCamera`: pitch -55..-60 (default -58), fixed yaw 0 (no rotation input), ground width across the screen 25-35 m at 400x150 at default zoom; zoom range only ~0.8-1.25x (wheel), no far strategic zoom.
- [ ] a2. Pan by edge-scroll (cursor within 2 cells of the edge), WASD/arrows and middle- or right-drag pan (grabbed ground stays under the cursor); focus clamped to the map; pan speed frame-rate independent (RE-03 tests cover it; browser: no jitter at 30 and 144 Hz caps).
- [ ] a3. ~200 units (default 200, `?n=` override 20..500) spread over the walkable hillside (seeded placement, no `Math.random`), 2 teams (100 each) by team colour through `engine.setTeamMaterials`; units drawn via `engine.instances` (F3 `instancedDraws` = parts per model x teams-or-groups, `instances` = 200); units stand on the terrain height; only facing (yaw) changes, no animation.
- [ ] a4. Placeholder unit model: a small upright voxel figure (not the lever), ~0.8-1 m wide, ~1.6-2 m tall, one `team.*` remap slot covering >= 40 % of its visible surface, so that at default zoom a unit is ~4-8 cells tall at 400x150 (measure and record the cell height at min/default/max zoom). Typical view shows 20-60 units on screen.
- [ ] a5. Left-click selects the front unit under the cursor (RE-04 `pickNearest`); left-drag draws a box (RE-07 screen rect) and selects all **own-team** units inside (RE-04 `selectInRect`); shift adds; click on empty ground clears. Only team 1 is selectable; enemy units are never selected.
- [ ] a6. Selected units show an RE-07 ground ring (hidden behind terrain/tower, visible on open ground); hovering a unit shows a thinner/different-colour ring; optional RE-07 health-bar row for selected units (full bar placeholder).
- [ ] a7. Node test(s) in `game/js/rts/`: selection rules (own team only, box ids ascending, shift-add, clear) on a fixture - run by `node tools/run-tests.mjs`.
- [ ] a8. `node tools/run-tests.mjs`, `node tools/check-deps.mjs` (game imports only `engine/index.js`) green; `?gpucompare=1` unchanged (spike adds no engine code; any engine change = separate RE row).
Acceptance criteria - **RTS-01b** (orders, perf, walk-test build):
- [ ] b1. Right-click on ground with a selection = move order: path per unit (or one shared path + per-unit offset) with RE-05 A* on a NavGrid (1 m cells, walkability from terrain slope + water, tower footprint blocked); a group of 30 arrives spread around the target (formation or RE-09 separation), none stuck on the tower or a steep slope in 5 scripted test orders.
- [ ] b2. Movement uses RE-09 separation (or a simple equivalent): no two units overlap > 20 % radius at rest; units face their move direction (yaw smoothed, <= 0.25 s turn); speed ~3-4 m/s; a right-click marker (brief ground ring or glyph, <= 0.5 s) shows the target.
- [ ] b3. Enemy team: idle, no AI; right-click on an enemy unit = move to it (no attack in the spike).
- [ ] b4. Sim runs on the fixed step (`STEP` from RE-14/loop.js), decoupled from render; no `Math.random`/`Date.now`/`performance.now` in `game/js/rts/sim/` (check-deps rule 15: 0 warnings there). Replay/lockstep hash **not** required for the spike.
- [ ] b5. Perf, owner Intel iGPU, 200 units, a 60-unit group moving on screen, `?renderer=mesh`: at **400x150 and 240x90** GPU p95 <= 4 ms (D-025 binding budget) and JS frame p95 <= 8 ms; **sim <= 2 ms** p95 (D-032); `over25 == 0`. Record the numbers in the sprint/gate note together with the delta vs the D-029 phase-1 walk numbers (2.52 / 1.34 ms). *Architect (2026-09-30): D-025 budget confirmed as the binding one (D-029's numbers are measurements, not a budget). Tightened: sim = sum of `simStep` per rendered frame, p95 <= 2 ms, and JS p95 includes it; units view + instance writes <= 0.1 ms; 2 instance groups (one per team). If RTS-01b is measured before RE-06b lands, a GPU miss at 400x150 is recorded (raster-pass p50 with/without units) and re-measured after RE-06b. It does not block the owner walk-test (readability is the gate). After RE-06b the 4 ms is binding.*
- [ ] b6. F3 shows: grid, renderer, units total / on screen, selected count, instancedDraws, sim ms, JS ms, GPU ms (p50/p95).
- [ ] b7. Node test: A* move of 30 units on a hillside-NavGrid fixture -> all reach within 3 m of the target in <= 600 steps, no unit inside a blocked cell.
- [ ] b8. Walk-test build: `game/rts-test.html` reachable from the dev page list/README with a 5-line "how to play" on screen (select, box, right-click, pan, zoom, F3); suites + check-deps green; one browser pass by the programmer.
- [ ] W. **Owner walk-test (exit)**, at min / default / max zoom, 400x150 and 240x90, answering D-032's questions: (1) can I **see** my units vs theirs, (2) can I **count** them, (3) can I **select** them (single click and box) and see which are selected, (4) does **moving a group feel good**. Result recorded as a **D-032 amendment**: GO -> PO (opus) + manager write the RTS GDD rewrite and milestone plan (new ADR), ME-12 replaced by the RTS-shaped phase-2 gate; NO-GO -> owner chooses (a) bigger glyph blocks + closer zoom re-spike (~1 d), (b) 2D top-down ASCII view, (c) back to the RPG.
**PO review (2026-09-30):** RTS-01a and RTS-01b = PO OK (see rows). **Blocker:** BUG-RTS-001 (ground reads as tiles) must be fixed (28.11a) before the walk-test, since it hurts readability judgement.
**Owner walk-test script** (server `python tools/serve.py`; open `http://localhost:9132/game/rts-test.html?f3=1`, then repeat with `&grid=240x90` or `?grid=240x90&f3=1`; use a big window for 400x150):
1. Look: is the ground continuous (no square patchwork)? Can you tell your team from the enemy at a glance, at default zoom, also in the distance/fog?
2. Camera: pan with WASD, screen edge and middle/right-drag (ground stays under cursor, no jitter); wheel zoom to min, default, max.
3. Count: at each zoom, can you estimate how many units are on screen (F3 shows the true number)? Are 4-10 cell tall figures still readable as units?
4. Select: click one unit, shift-click more, click empty ground to clear. Is the selection ring clear, also on slopes and next to the tower (hidden behind terrain)? Hover ring visible?
5. Box select: drag a box over a mixed crowd; only your team is selected, count matches F3.
6. Move: right-click the ground with 5, then 30+ selected units (also across the hill and past the tower). Do they arrive spread, face their way, not clump or get stuck? Does it feel good (speed 3.5 m/s, turn, marker)?
7. Perf: F3 at 400x150 and 240x90 with 60 units moving: GPU p95 <= 4 ms, JS <= 8 ms, over25 = 0 on your Intel iGPU; note the numbers.
8. Repeat 1-6 at 240x90; note which grid you would play.
**Recording:** owner answers the four D-032 questions (see / count / select / move feels good) per grid; the main session writes the D-032 amendment: GO (-> PO opus + manager, RTS GDD + milestone plan) or NO-GO with option (a) bigger glyph blocks + closer zoom re-spike, (b) 2D top-down ASCII, (c) back to RPG.
**Out of scope:** economy/gathering, buildings/placement, production, combat/attack/health logic, AI, fog of war (RE-11/12), minimap (RE-13), control groups/hotkeys, sound, animation beyond facing, camera rotation, far zoom, lockstep/replay, save/load, unit variety (one model), new engine code (gaps -> new RE rows / ASK ARCHITECT).
Design needed: optional. Placeholder unit model + placeholder team materials are built by the programmer; designer asked for unit glyph variants only if the placeholders fail readability (D-032 consequences).
Notes / dependencies: D-032 item 2 (gate), items 1/5 (frozen list, determinism rule); architecture.md 28.1 (pitched camera, sign rule pitch -58), 28.2 (nav), 28.6 (`engine.instances`, team remap, `?units=N` harness to copy from). Architect: confirm whether the RE rows' notes (28.1-28.6) are enough as the D-032 "spike tech notes" or add a short `game/js/rts/` layout note (sim SoA, tick vs render, input -> orders). **Architect (2026-09-30): layout note = architecture.md 28.8 (normative).** Prerequisites before dev: RE-02, RE-03 fixes, RE-EXP, and RE-07 for a5/a6. RE-06b is not a prerequisite.

## Epic RE "RTS engine capability" (architect 2026-09-30; D-032, D-029, D-006/D-014; sketches)
**Scope:** genre-neutral engine features an RTS needs; unit/command/economy/AI logic stays in `game/js/rts/`. **Phasing:** RE-01..07 are **RTS-01 prerequisites** (the spike may take thin versions of RE-04/05/07 in-story, then these rows harden them); RE-08..15 after a spike PASS; RE-16 closes the epic (= input to the RTS-shaped phase-2 gate, D-032 item 4). **Camera verdict:** the y-shear camera cannot do a 55-60 deg down-look (vertical image plane: across a 30 m view the horizontal scale changes ~3.5x top to bottom, vs ~1.4x for a real pitched camera) -> a real view matrix on the mesh path only (`cam.projection: 'pitched'`; DDA rejects it); amends architecture.md 27.1 item 2 / 27.5 for that mode (architect note, no manager entry). **Tech note before dev:** RE-01+02 (camera, one note), RE-05/08/09 (`engine/nav/` layout + determinism rules), RE-06 (instancing), RE-11+12 (fog), RE-14 (commands/replay). Sim rules for every RE row: fixed step, seeded RNG, iteration by id, zero per-frame allocation, `// @ts-check`.

Node 155/155 (1 pre-existing unrelated FAIL in `tools/vox-split.test.mjs`, ME-22's in-flight work), check-deps OK, 500-instance cull+compact measured 0.049 ms (budget 0.1 ms). **NEEDS ARCHITECT:** (1) no existing engine-wide "rendered frame" counter reaches compositor.js/GpuCellPipeline.js, so `frameNo` is each caller's OWN counter (`meshInstFrameNo` / `this._frameNo`, incremented once per actual twin render) rather than one shared source - they stay in lockstep only because every pose in the current gpucompare run calls both twins exactly once (verified for the one RE-06 instanced pose); an uneven call ratio would desync them (safe fallback: recompute, not incorrect reuse) but never actually hit it - please confirm this is acceptable or if a shared frame counter should be threaded through `fb` instead. (2) `DrawList.addInstances` still separately calls `groupRadius` to build its own item aabb, so R is computed twice per group per frame (once for the per-instance cull, once inside `addInstances`) - correctness is fine, but not literally "once" as point 2 reads; flag in case RE-15c's hysteresis wants a single R source. **RE-15b dev done -> arch-review (PC-B, 2026-10-01):** `engine/mesh/voxelMesh.js` - `VoxelMeshCache.get(pm, key, names, lod=0)` now takes `lod`; `lod=1` lazily builds+caches (own `WeakMap`, id `vox:<key>@1`) a 2x2x2-downsampled mesh via two new exported helpers, `downsamplePart(pm, p)` (any-solid -> block solid; most-frequent local mat, tie -> lowest id) and `buildVoxelMeshLod1(pm, opts)`. LOD1 reuses the SAME `emitPartFaces`/greedy-mesher unchanged: a thin `ScaledMeshBuilder` proxy scales only the emitted quad corners x2 so vertices land in LOD0's part-local space (half-res box-local index i -> LOD0 index 2i), per 28.13 point 5. `parts`/part names/ranges identical to LOD0. Build logs ms and warns over the 5 ms budget; measured quadruped12 0.33-1.26 ms, lever 0.57-0.63 ms (well under). Tests added to `voxelMesh.test.js` (8 new cases incl. direct `downsamplePart` unit checks for the any-solid and tie-break rules, bbox-within-+1-cell, strict quad-count-< on quadruped12, cached identity, lever timing). Node 208/208 in this file, `run-tests.mjs` 154/155 (1 pre-existing typecheck WARN, same baseline), `check-deps.mjs` OK, no new warnings. **NEEDS ARCHITECT:** the "R = max over both LOD meshes' bboxes" rule (point 5/6) is NOT wired up anywhere yet - `groupRadius(mesh, parts)` in `engine/mesh/instances.js` takes one mesh, and its one call site (the per-instance-cull R, instances.js ~line 256) only ever has the LOD0 mesh in scope today (LOD1 isn't fetched until RE-15c's selection logic exists). I left `instances.js` untouched per this story's scope. LOD1's `mesh.bbox` is correctly computed (StaticMeshBuilder tracks it from the actual scaled positions), so RE-15c can compute `R = max(groupRadius(meshLod0, parts), groupRadius(meshLod1, parts))` at that call site with no further change needed in voxelMesh.js - flagging so RE-15c's tech note/implementation doesn't silently use LOD0-only R. | RE-15a [PC-B, cross-track -> arch-review] cull + compaction + frameNo memo + F3 stats (engine/mesh/instances.js, DrawList.js, 1-line callers) 0.5 d; RE-15b [PC-B -> arch-review] LOD1 mesh build + cache (voxelMesh.js) 0.5 d; RE-15c [PC-A] LOD selection + hysteresis + 2 items/group + unitsView/harness opt-in + gpucompare `unitsCullLod` + bench 0.5 d. deps: RE-06/06b/06c (ME-17 not needed). ACs: (1) off-screen instances cost 0 draws, survivors in game order, bit-identical words; (2) cull+LOD+compaction <= 0.1 ms for 500, zero alloc; (3) second twin call in a frame reuses the result; (4) existing gpucompare poses identical incl. unitsInstanced*; (5) `?bench=1&units=200&lod=8` vs 0 at 400x150 raster p50 delta <= 0.65 ms (goal 0.44), RTS view <= 0.44; (6) F3 `inst drawn/total lod1 cull`. **RE-15c ARCH OK (opus 2026-10-02; nits a/b applied; 28.13 amendment) - RE-15 stays open for RE-15d (RTS bench).** Dev: per-instance LOD0/LOD1 with 0.9/1.1 hysteresis, 2 draw items per group, opt-in `group.lodCells` (RTS unitsView 8, `?lod=`; harness `?units=N&lod=C`), `addInstances` 5th arg R = max(LOD0, LOD1), `addToDrawList(..., viewProj, rows)`; gpucompare pose `unitsCullLod` (drawn 36 / culled 24 / lod1 14); mesh 55/55, dda 35/35, Node 177/177. Bench (this PC, Intel Arc iGPU) raster p50 delta units=200&lod=8: ground +0.885 (over 0.65 bar), mid +0.364, summit +0.005, waystone +0.516 (no-lod +0.926); RTS bench blocked by BUG-RTS-002. |

## Epic EP-ELEMENTS "Particles, water, fire, wind, weather" (PO 2026-10-01, owner request; sketches, see `### US-053a` onwards near the bottom of the file)
**Scope:** particles, liquid surfaces, burning, fire spread, explosions, wind as a real force, weather/fog. Engine parts stay genre-neutral (D-006); which things burn, what explodes and the tuning numbers are content data. **Existing rows are linked, not duplicated:** US-053 (particles) and US-055 (water) stay the umbrella rows and close when their steps below are done; floating props stay in US-055 after US-051. US-093 (torch burns barriers/brush) and US-101 (Spark lights braziers) use this epic; US-082 (combat feel) uses the particles; US-121 "wind grass" stays a visual shader preset that will read the US-138 wind uniform; US-122 (day/night) and US-127 (light in fog) pair with US-139/140. **Object physics (US-051/052) is not a blocker:** every story here first works on the player capsule, entities and static world data; "pushes / ignites / floats physics bodies" is a later AC marked *(after US-051)*. **Phasing:** particles (053a-c) first, they feed everything else -> water (055a/b) and fire (132-135) -> explosions (136/137) -> wind (138) -> weather (139/140, stretch). **Tech note before dev (NEEDS PC-A: architect note):** US-053a+053b (one note: module home e.g. `engine/fx/`, pool layout, sprite-pass draw, JS twin), US-055a, US-132+133 (one note: status-effect component, flammability on materials vs entities, fire grid), US-136, US-138, US-139. Sim rules for every row: fixed step, seeded RNG, iteration by id, zero per-frame allocation, save-safe state where it persists.

### US-078 Sword swing + hit detection  [Priority: P0 (M3 opener)] [Status: todo (sketch)]  [PC-A engine / PC-B game]
As a player, I want to swing a sword with the mouse and have it hit what it visibly touches, so that the world outside the tower can be fought in, not just walked.
Acceptance criteria:
- [ ] **Getting it (PO 2026-10-01, D-030 amendment 1 Q3):** the ruin-steel sword lies inside the tower at a designer-placed spot (a rack, plinth or the old guard's remains; not on the hillside), reachable from the wake spot without leaving the tower (`[E] Take sword`). Before that, attack input does nothing. Taking it sets a save-safe flag and the prop disappears.
- [ ] **Swing (owner 2026-10-01, D-034: one swing motion, light + hard):** left mouse (action `attack`). **Tap = light swing:** 0.35 s (windup 0.08 / active 0.12 / recover 0.15), damage 1 (x `damageScale`), no cost; pressing again during recover queues one more light swing (same motion), max 2, then 0.25 s rest. **Hold >= 0.4 s then release = hard swing:** the wind-up pose is held and shown while charging; release swings with damage x3, knockback + stagger (interrupts a beast windup/charge, pushes it ~1.5 m), costs 4 mana (not allowed / falls back to a light swing below 4 mana, with the US-080b mana-short flash); slower and risky: recover 0.45 s, walk x0.3 while charging and recovering. Walk speed x0.6 during a light swing; no swing in mid-air in M3. Numbers are placeholders for the owner feel check.
- [ ] **View model:** the sword is drawn in the lower right of the view as a first-person model with idle sway, a readable swing arc (>= 5 poses per swing) and never clips into walls (drawn in its own depth range). Readable at 160x60 and 240x90. With the sword taken, the carried light moves to the left side (0.3 m left instead of right, GDD 7.3).
- [ ] **Hit detection:** during the active window only, a hit arc in front of the eye: reach 1.6 m, 100 deg horizontal, from 1.0 m below to 0.3 m above eye height. Targets are entity hurt spheres; a target is hit at most once per swing; a target behind world geometry (ray from the eye to the hit point blocked) is not hit.
- [ ] **Hit event:** each hit sends `hit {source, target, damage: 1, dir, point}` to the target's behaviour. A test target (designer: a mossy practice stump or old armour stand near the sword) flashes white for 0.1 s and shows a 3-cell spark at the hit point.
- [ ] **World hit:** if the arc meets a wall/terrain within reach first, the swing stops early with a `*` spark for 0.1 s and 0.05 s hit-stop ("clink").
- [ ] **Hit event (PO 2026-10-01):** hits are also emitted as `combat:hit {source:'player', target, damage:1}` on `engine.events` (architecture.md 29.1) so US-080 and US-079a share one channel.
- [ ] **Block/parry hook (PO 2026-10-01, Q1):** the swing state exposes `canSwing()` and an `onBlockStart/onBlockEnd` seam so the shield (US-086) can cancel a swing and block; no shield logic in this story.
- [ ] **Tests:** Node tests for the arc query (in arc = hit, outside angle/reach = miss, behind a wall = miss, one hit per swing, chain timing) at the fixed 60 Hz step, deterministic. Hit query <= 0.1 ms with 16 entities, no per-frame allocation.
Design needed: yes - sword view model (idle + 2 swing directions, >= 5 poses each), world sword prop placed in the tower, practice target, spark glyphs (designer). **NEEDS PC-A: Designer done 2026-10-01 (preview verified by the main session: 39 checks PASS, no console errors): design/models/sword.js (voxelModels.sword pickup + swordHeld + viewModels.sword idle/swingLR/swingRL/trail/sparks, README 7.4), preview design/preview/sword.html; placement = levelPatch.towerSword (sword (12.86, 6.12, z 0.9) facing 110 in rubble heap cell 12,6, [E] Take sword, flag tower.sword.taken, scrawl STEEL FOR THE HUSH for the writer). Still NEEDS PC-A: swordKit merge (3 colours + 6 materials into palette.js/detail-pass.js, script tag), practice-target model (spot 14.6, 6.6), architect note for the view-model layer (owner: no hand/glove drawn, Wick never seen - OK?), architect note (PO 2026-10-01).**
Notes / dependencies: **needs architect notes (engine: hitboxes/hurt spheres, arc query in `World`, view-model render layer on the mesh pass, event routing).** **Needs designer (sword/enemy art).** After the phase-2 gate (ME-11 colliders) and ME-19 at best; can prototype on the current renderer if the architect agrees. US-082 adds the full feel; sounds wait for M6.

### US-079 First enemy with simple AI (Hush-touched beast)  [Priority: P0 (M3 opener)] [Status: todo (sketch)]  [PC-A engine / PC-B brain + content]
As a player, I want to meet a beast that notices me, warns me, charges and can be beaten, so that the sword has a purpose and the wild outside the Wall feels dangerous.
Acceptance criteria:
- [ ] **Model:** a creature about 1.0 m long and 0.7 m tall (working: a Hush-touched boar; owner question 2) with clips idle, walk, run, windup, charge, hurt, die (US-041b / US-083). Silhouette readable at 10 m at 160x60.
- [ ] **Stats:** 3 HP (3 sword hits), contact damage 1 heart, walk 1.5 m/s, chase 4.5 m/s, charge 7 m/s. Same capsule physics as the player (no climbing above step-up, turns back at drops > 1 m).
- [ ] **States:** `wander` (within 6 m of home, 2-4 s pauses) -> `notice` (player within 12 m inside a 120 deg view cone with line of sight, or within 3 m in any direction; 0.6 s pause with a `!` above its head) -> `chase` -> `windup` at 5 m (0.5 s, visible scrape + lowered head) -> `charge` (straight line up to 1.2 s or until it hits a wall/the player) -> `recover` (1.0 s, 2.0 s after a wall hit = open to hits) -> back to `chase`. `return` home and heal to full when the player is > 20 m away or out of sight for 5 s.
- [ ] **Hurt/die:** a hit flashes it white 0.1 s, flinches it 0.25 s and knocks it back 1.5 m; a hit during `windup` cancels the charge. At 0 HP: die clip 0.6 s, then it fades into dust over 0.5 s and is removed; 50 % chance to drop a heart (US-080).
- [ ] **Light:** it never enters a woken relay's light radius (safe zone, pillar "light is life").
- [ ] **Content:** 2 beasts placed on the hillside as `entities[]` in content JSON (`brain: "beast"`), none within 15 m of the breach. Killed beasts come back after a relay rest or a reload.
- [ ] **Tests + budget:** Node tests drive every state transition with scripted player positions; a 600-step replay is deterministic. AI for 8 beasts <= 0.3 ms/frame JS, no per-frame allocation.
Design needed: yes - beast model + 7 clips, `!` alert glyph, dust fade (designer). Writer: the beast's name.
Notes / dependencies: **needs architect notes (engine: AI/brain component hooks, perception ray, entity spawn/despawn + respawn state).** **Needs designer (sword/enemy art).** After US-078, US-041b; uses US-080 for damage. US-085 later generalises the brain; US-084 pathfinding is not needed here (direct steering + edge check). **2026-09-30:** superseded. The chase uses RE-05/09 nav (see US-079a), so the states + pathing ACs move there and this story keeps model/clips, hurt/die, drops, light safe zone and respawn.

### US-080 Hearts, damage, death + respawn  [Priority: P0 (M3 opener)] [Status: todo (sketch)]  [PC-B HUD/quest / PC-A health component]
**Rewritten (PO 2026-10-01, D-030 amendment 1 Q7/Q9): HP + mana bars, no hearts. Split: US-080a = HP, damage, death/respawn (ACs 1-5, 8); US-080b = mana + pickups + dev keys (ACs 6, 7).**
As a player, I want to see my HP and mana bars, lose HP when I get hit and wake again when I fall, so that fights have stakes but never cost me my progress.
Acceptance criteria:
- [ ] **1. HP bar (080a):** top-left bar, 20 cells wide (designer glyph art, ASCII 32-126 only, `[####....]` style), a number `hp/max` beside it, start 30/30. Readable at 160x60 and 240x90, hidden during the title, map and end cards. At <= 25 % the bar pulses red at 1 Hz.
- [ ] **2. Health component (080a):** player `health {hp, max}` as an entity component in the serialized state (survives save/load, CO-5); `max` is raised later by US-095 vessels.
- [ ] **3. Damage via events (080a):** a listener on `engine.events` `combat:hit {source, target, damage}` (architecture.md 29.1) applies `damage` to `target` when the target is the player; US-079a's beast already emits it (1 -> scaled to 5 HP by a `damageScale` constant, noted in the row). Falls > 6 m = 5 HP, > 10 m = 10 HP (changes GDD 5 "no fall damage"). On damage: 1.0 s invulnerability, red screen-edge flash 0.15 s, knockback 2 m/s away from the source, 2 deg camera kick.
- [ ] **4. Death + respawn (080a, D-030 amendment 1 Q9):** at 0 HP controls lock, the eye sinks to 0.4 m over 0.8 s, fade to black 1.5 s, card (writer text) with `[E] Wake again`. Respawn at the last save point (a woken relay; the tower wake spot until one exists, plus the autosave position once US-089 exists) with full HP and mana; enemies reset to home; world flags (torch, lever, sword, chests) are kept.
- [ ] **5. Dev (080a):** with `?debug=1`, one key toggles invulnerability and one deals 5 HP to the player.
- [ ] **6. Mana bar (080b):** a second bar below the HP bar in blue, 20 cells, `mp/max`, start 20/20 (`mana {mp, max}` component, serialized). `spendMana(n)` returns false and flashes the bar when short; regenerates 1 per 2 s, paused 3 s after spending. Nothing spends mana yet (D-021 spells later).
- [ ] **7. Pickups (080b):** a red restore-HP drop (+10 HP) and a blue restore-mana drop (+10 MP): they bob, are collected within 0.8 m, despawn after 20 s (blink the last 3 s); the beast drops one with 50 % chance (US-079a hook).
- [ ] **8. Tests (080a/b):** Node tests for damage/invulnerability timing, fall thresholds, death -> respawn (flags kept, HP and mana full, enemies reset), save round trip of `health` and `mana`, mana regen/spend timing, deterministic at the 60 Hz step.
Design needed: yes - HP/mana bar glyph art (frame, fill, low-pulse), pickup drops, damage flash colour, death card style (designer); death card text (writer).
Notes / dependencies: **needs architect notes (engine: `health` component + damage events, respawn hook in `World`).** **Needs designer (heart/HUD art).** Pairs with US-079; HP/mana upgrades come in US-095; relay save + autosave = US-089. **ACs rewritten 2026-10-01 per D-033 (PO).** **NEEDS PC-A: designer HP/mana bar art + architect note (health/mana components, respawn hook).**

### US-079a First beast, brain + nav chase  [Priority: P0 (M3, sprint 5)] [Status: dev done -> arch-review]  [PC-B brain + content / PC-A architect note]
As a player, I want a beast on the hillside that notices me, finds its way around rocks and the tower, and charges me, so that the world outside feels alive and dangerous before the sword exists.
Acceptance criteria:
- [x] 1. **Placeholder model:** a simple voxel boar block (~1.0 m long, 0.7 m tall), readable as an animal at 10 m at 240x90. The programmer may build it; final art + clips = US-079 / US-041b. Only facing changes, no clips.
- [x] 2. **States** (numbers from US-079): `wander` (<= 6 m from home, 2-4 s pauses, walk 1.5 m/s) -> `notice` (player <= 12 m inside a 120 deg cone with line of sight, or <= 3 m any direction; 0.6 s pause, `!` above its head drawn as an overlay marker) -> `chase` (4.5 m/s) -> `windup` at 5 m (0.5 s) -> `charge` (7 m/s straight line, <= 1.2 s or until it hits a wall/the player) -> `recover` (1.0 s, 2.0 s after a wall hit) -> `chase`. `return` home when the player is > 20 m away or out of sight for 5 s; `return` walks at 1.5 m/s and may re-notice the player on the way (PO 2026-10-01).
- [x] 3. **Pathing:** `chase`/`return` follow an RE-05 A* path on a 1 m NavGrid (walkability from the terrain slope + water + tower footprint blocked; setup like `game/js/rts/sim/navSetup.js`, placed where the architect says). Re-path at most every 0.5 s or when the player moves > 2 m. RE-09 steering keeps 2 beasts from overlapping. In 5 scripted chases from the far side of the tower, the beast reaches the player without getting stuck. It never walks down a drop > 1 m.
- [x] 4. **Ground:** the beast stands on the terrain/mesh height (`supportAt` on `physics=mesh`) with no floating or sinking > 0.1 m on the hillside.
- [x] 5. **Contact:** when a charge reaches the player, a `combat:hit {source, target, damage: 1}` event is emitted on `engine.events` (no-op until US-080) (PO 2026-10-01). No player damage in this story.
- [x] 6. **Content:** 2 beasts in content JSON `entities[]` (`components.brain {kind: "beast", home}` + `components.targetable {radius, height}`) (PO 2026-10-01), none within 15 m of the breach, placed on the hillside route before the waystone.
- [x] 7. **Determinism + budget:** fixed step, seeded RNG, no wall clock (D-032 item 5). Node tests drive every state change with scripted player positions. The 600-step replay is bit-equal on two runs within one world load (not cross-machine, `groundAt`) (PO 2026-10-01). 8 beasts <= 0.3 ms/frame JS incl. pathing (paths amortised), zero per-frame allocation.
- [x] 8. `node tools/run-tests.mjs`, `node tools/check-deps.mjs` (game imports only `engine/index.js`, never `game/js/rts/`) green; `?gpucompare=1` unchanged. One browser pass on `?renderer=mesh&physics=mesh`.
Design needed: no (placeholder). Designer boar + clips come with US-079.
Notes / dependencies: dev only after the ME-12 GO. **NEEDS PC-A: architect note** (where the brain lives: `game/js/quest/` vs an engine `brain` hook, perception ray API, entity spawn from content, and whether the walkability setup moves from `game/js/rts/` into `engine/nav/`). Owner check: "does it feel like it hunts me?"

**dev done -> arch-review (PC-B, 2026-10-01).** Full SoA state machine per architecture.md 29.1: `game/js/quest/sim/beastConfig.js` (`BEAST_DEFAULTS`/`toSteps`), `sim/sight.js` (`canSee`, chunks any >5m segment into <=5m `hasLineOfSight` calls to keep the engine's 20-sample cap at 0.25m spacing), `sim/beastNav.js` (`buildBeastNav` wrapping `NavGrid.buildFromWorld` + A*, using RE-05c's `maxStepM`), `sim/beastSim.js` (`createBeastSim`, zero-alloc `step/hashInto/save/load/resetAll`), `beastView.js` (presentation-only, `yawFromDelta` not raw `atan2`, draws the `!` notice marker). `design/models/voxel_beast.js`: placeholder `boarPlaceholder` (0.5x1.0x0.7m, reuses already-merged `leather`/`canvas_dark` materials, no palette/detail-pass merge needed). `content/worlds/world_m1.world.json`: world-level `nav` block + `boar1`/`boar2` entities (~26m/~43m from the breach, verified walkable/flat). `game/js/quest/sim/beastSim.test.js`: 19 assertions (notice cone in/out, LOS-blocked unseen-timer return, >20m immediate return, wall-hit recovery, exactly-one-`combat:hit` contact, 5 scripted chases from 4 tower corners + 1 depth crossing all reaching contact <=30s never in a cost-0 cell and z within 0.1m of `supportAt`, 600-step determinism replay, save@300/load/same-hash@600, the RE-05c drop rule through `buildFromArrays`). Perf (8 beasts, worst case all chasing): mean 0.014 ms/step, p95 0.041 ms - well inside the 0.1/0.3 ms budget; zero-alloc heap check over 10k steps passes.

**Main session wiring** (not per-story scope, applied after both parallel agents finished): `game/index.html` 1 `<script>` line for `voxel_beast.js`; `game/js/main.js` - `createRng` added to the engine import, `createBeastSim`/`presentBeasts`/`questOverlayStyles` imports, `beasts` module-level var, `beasts = createBeastSim(world, {nav: worldDef.nav, rng: createRng(worldDef.nav?.seed ?? 1), events: engine.events})` in `'world:loaded'` next to `playerHandle`, `beasts.step(pt.x, pt.y, pt.z)` after `resolveBodyContacts`, `engine.overlay.clear()` + `presentBeasts(...)` right before the RE-07 overlay flush, `engine.overlay.setStyles(questOverlayStyles(assets.uiStyle))` + a resolved `ovlStyles = {beastNotice: engine.overlay.styleId('beastNotice')}` at boot. New `game/js/quest/overlayStyles.js` (`questOverlayStyles(uiStyle)` forwards `uiStyle.overlay` as-is - no style invented, just the one call site 29.1 asked for) and one placeholder `beastNotice` style (`!`, alert yellow) added to `design/models/title.js`'s existing `A.uiStyle.overlay` block (the US-128 designer styles were already there; `beastNotice` was the missing one blocking this wiring).

**Deviations flagged by the programmer** (none silent): (1) the "behind the tower"/"hidden 5s" ACs collapse into one test scenario - geometrically, no two points on opposite sides of the 24x14m tower footprint can both be within the 12m noticeR, so only the 20m loseR path can ever exercise occlusion; (2) `stats.astarNodes` approximates accumulated path length, not a true expanded-node count (`engine/nav/astar.js` never exposes that number); (3) two tuning constants not in the AC's numbered list (`DEFAULT_ACCEL 12 m/s^2` for non-charge states, wander/travel `maxNodes` 500/3000 split) and the charge wall-slow-streak counter reuses the otherwise-unused `unseen` field rather than adding a new SoA array.

Verification: `node game/js/quest/sim/beastSim.test.js` 19/19; `node tools/run-tests.mjs` green (1 pre-existing unrelated FAIL in `engine/mesh/shadowList.test.js`); `node tools/check-deps.mjs` OK, no new warnings (the beast `sim/**` files are clean on rule 15). Headless `tools/capture-browser.mjs --mode gpucompare` smoke-passed on both the default renderer and `?renderer=mesh` with the real world_m1 content (beasts + sword + overlay wiring all live in the render loop) - all poses PASS, no console errors - standing in for AC 8's "one browser pass" (no interactive Chrome available in this sandbox).

### US-128 Z-targeting (lock-on)  [Priority: P1 (M3, sprint 5)] [Status: po-review]  [PC-A camera + architect note / PC-B game wiring]
As a player, I want to lock on to an enemy with one key and keep it in view, so that first-person melee stays readable when the beast circles or charges.

**US-128b programmer report (2026-10-02).** `game/js/quest/targeting.js` (`createTargeting(world, events, cfg)`:
`step`/`present`/`clear`) + `targeting.test.js` (43 checks: centre-weighting, range 15/break 20 edges, LOS-hidden
skip + 60-vs-54-step break timing, cycle order+wrap, dead/removed breaks lock, `noTargetT`, present() ring/bar/fade/
no-target ops, perf <=0.05 ms + zero-alloc over 10k steps). `node tools/run-tests.mjs`/`check-deps.mjs` green (same
2 pre-existing unrelated FAILs + 1 WARN as baseline, nothing new). `engine/ui/overlay.test.js` unchanged (21/21).
Deviation (reasonable call, not a silent guess): the FOV test uses a horizontal dot product against a
`cos(hfovDeg/2)` threshold computed once at create time, not an atan2 yaw-offset per candidate per step (yaw-offset
math is used, as 29.2 says, for the Tab/wheel cycle - an occasional, not per-step, path). AC1-6 implemented; AC7
(owner feel check) and part of AC8 (the mesh-renderer walk) still need the main.js wiring below + an owner pass.
**main.js wiring (5 lines, main session applies):**
1. import: `import { createTargeting } from './quest/targeting.js';`
2. after world load (next to `beasts =`/`vitals =`): `targeting = createTargeting(world, engine.events, {});` then
   pass it into vitals' hooks (`createVitals(..., { beasts, targeting })`, replacing the current `targeting: null`).
3. in `update`, before `look.update(dt)`: `if (targeting) targeting.step(dt, input.pressed('KeyQ'), cycleDir(input), playerHandle.data, look);`
   where `cycleDir(input) = (input.pressed('Tab') ? (input.isDown('ShiftLeft') || input.isDown('ShiftRight') ? -1 : 1) : 0) + input.consumeWheel();`
4. in `render`, after `presentBeasts(...)`: `if (targeting) targeting.present(engine.overlay, ovlStyles);`
5. boot, next to the existing `engine.overlay.setStyles(...)`/`ovlStyles` lines: extend `ovlStyles` with
   `target: engine.overlay.styleId('target')`, `targetFade: engine.overlay.styleId('targetFade')`,
   `targetNone: engine.overlay.styleId('targetNone')`, `targetBarFill: engine.overlay.styleId('targetBarFill')`,
   `targetBarEmpty: engine.overlay.styleId('targetBarEmpty')` (styles already in `design/models/title.js`); also
   `engine.overlay.setGroundFn((x, y) => world.terrain.groundAt(x, y))` once at boot (terrain always exists outdoors).

Acceptance criteria:
- [ ] 1. **Lock:** `Q` (action `lock` once US-087 exists) locks the best target: the entity with a `targetable` flag that is <= 15 m away, in line of sight, and closest to the screen centre. Pressing `Q` again unlocks (toggle; hold mode = a later settings option). No target in range = a short "no target" tick (two 1-cell `x` at the crosshair +-2 cols, style `targetNone`, 0.3 s), no lock (PO 2026-10-01).
- [ ] 2. **Ring + bar:** a locked target shows an `engine.overlay` ground ring under it (radius = target radius + 0.2 m; designer colour, placeholder `ui.target`). It follows slopes and is hidden behind terrain/walls like the RE-07 rings. A small bar above it shows its HP when it has `health` (full bar placeholder until US-080).
- [ ] 3. **Camera:** while locked, the view yaw turns toward the target at <= 360 deg/s (pitch toward it, clamped to the pitched 70 deg clamp). Mouse look is damped to 25 % and becomes an offset around the target, clamped to +-20 deg yaw / +-10 deg pitch, no decay (PO 2026-10-01; owner tunes at the feel check), and movement becomes strafe/circle around the target.
- [ ] 4. **Cycle:** mouse wheel or `Tab` switches to the next valid target left/right of the current one. The ring moves at once.
- [ ] 5. **Break:** the lock drops when the target dies/despawns, goes > 20 m, or stays out of line of sight > 1.0 s. The ring fades out in 0.2 s (overlay has no alpha: the ring is drawn 0.2 s in the dimmer `targetFade` colour, PO 2026-10-01). Designer done (2026-10-01): styles in design/models/title.js ASSETS.uiStyle.overlay - target, targetFade, targetNone, targetBarFill, targetBarEmpty (29.2 ids); pass the object to engine.overlay.setStyles (merge with any other setStyles call) (placeholders fine for dev).
- [ ] 6. **Tests:** Node tests for target choice (centre-weighted, range, line of sight), cycling order, break rules and the yaw turn rate at the fixed step. Target query for 16 entities <= 0.05 ms, zero allocation.
- [ ] 7. **Owner feel check** on `?renderer=mesh` with the US-079a beasts: locking reads clearly at 240x90 and 400x150, and the camera turn does not make you sick.
- [ ] 8. Suites + check-deps green. Overlay tests + `rtsOverlay` gpucompare pose unchanged.
Design needed: yes, small. Lock ring colour/glyph + "no target" tick (designer `ui.target`); a placeholder is fine for dev.
Notes / dependencies: after US-079a. Uses RE-07 overlay (engine, done) and RE-04 pick/line-of-sight if useful. **NEEDS PC-A: architect note** (lock-on camera turn in `PlayerLook` as an option vs game-side, overlay ring API for a first-person view). Replaces the US-081 sketch.

### US-020 Sound  [Priority: P2] [Status: todo]
As a player, I want to hear the fire crackle, my steps, the boulder and the wind, so that the space feels real.
Acceptance criteria:

- [ ] Audio context starts after the first user input (no autoplay errors in the console).
- [ ] Sounds per GDD 7.5: brazier crackle attenuates with distance; footsteps on stone with slight pitch variation; boulder roll + thud; lever clunk; grate rattle; wind grows near the breach.
- [ ] `N` key toggles mute. (Was `M`; `M` now opens the map card, US-015/D-011. In M2 mute also moves into Settings, US-038.)

### US-021 Relay-keeper's log (was: readable wall scrawl)  [Priority: P2] [Status: design]
As a player, I want to find an old relay-keeper's log scratched into the stone near where I came to, so that I learn someone out here heard the signal long before me.

**D-011 reskin (PO, 2026-09-23):** `KEEP THE LIGHT` is replaced by the relay-keeper's log (`docs/story.md` section 4, 5 lines, each <= 60 chars). 60-char lines cannot be read as a wall decal at the M1 grids, so the log is a **visible decal plus a read panel**.
Acceptance criteria:
- [ ] Decal material: rows of scratched tally marks and letters on one wall panel near the wake spot (about 1.5 m wide), clearly "writing" from 2 m, fading into the stone texture at > 5 m. It does not need to be legible as text.
- [ ] An interactable on the decal (US-012 rules) shows `[E] Read`. Pressing E opens the log text in the US-015 panel overlay (same dim and fade, style `uiStyle.logPanel`: scratched-stone plate, pale grey text); any key closes it. Readable at 160x60 and 240x90.
- [ ] The text comes from level/UI data, not code. It matches `docs/story.md` section 4 exactly and does not name Wick (D-013). It keeps "the SOS has been sending for years" vague (D-013 item 3).
- [ ] Reading is optional: nothing else depends on it. Restart resets nothing here (the log can be read any number of times).
Design needed: yes – decal glyph pattern + log panel style (low priority for designer).
Notes / dependencies: US-004, US-010, US-012 (interaction), US-015 (panel overlay).

### US-023 See-through grate (masked walls)  [Priority: P2] [Status: todo]
As a player, I want to see the upper stair through the bars of the grate, so that the portcullis looks like real ironwork and hints at the way up.
Acceptance criteria:
- [ ] Wall texels flagged `hole: true` in a material (the `grate` material) are transparent. The ray continues behind the masked face, and the geometry and sky behind it are drawn through the gaps.
- [ ] Works while the grate animates (US-014): the masked face shrinks as `ceilH` rises, with no tearing at the moving edge.
- [ ] Sprites behind the grate are occluded only by the bar texels, not by the gaps.
- [ ] Cost: at most 0.5 ms extra per frame when the grate fills the view; US-018 still holds.
Design needed: no (the `grate` material with `hole` texels was delivered with US-011).
Notes / dependencies: US-004, US-011, US-014. Picked up only after every P0 story is `done`. Not an M1 exit criterion. Until then the M1 fallback draws the gaps dark (US-011).

## Milestone 2 "First Steps" – sketches (not yet refined; not M1 scope)

### US-026 Walk out onto the terrain: near LOD, slope physics, chunk regeneration  [Priority: P0 (M2)] [Status: todo]
As a player, I want to step out of the breach and keep walking down the hill, so that the open world is really open.
Acceptance criteria (sketch):
- [ ] `terrainCaster` near LOD: 2 m cells within ~300 m with distance-scaled step LOD, the US-016b near look, no seam with the 8 m far LOD (same function), and the combined terrain pass <= 3 ms.
- [ ] `engine/physics/terrainCollide.js`: capsule and sphere stand on bilinear terrain height. Slopes up to 50 degrees are walkable; steeper slopes make you slide. The structure-to-terrain handover at the tower ring has no bump (US-016b blend = 0).
- [ ] Chunk regeneration as the player moves (3x3 resident) with no frame hitch above 25 ms.
- [ ] The M1 end trigger is replaced (M2 flag) by a seamless walk-out. The M1 fade stays available behind a flag for the M1 build.
- [ ] Terrain lighting: ambient + sun N.L from grid normals; point lights within radius; no terrain shadow rays.
Design needed: yes (US-016b near look; walkable path detail near the tower).
Notes / dependencies: US-025, US-016b, US-016.

### US-026a Bounded walk-out: step from the breach onto real terrain, end card at a terrain marker  [Priority: P0 (M2)] [Status: S1-S6 done, PO OK (2026-09-26) -> `testing`; split US-026a-engine PC-A / US-026a-content PC-B; US-026a-engine S1+S2 done (programmer); S3 done (programmer); S4 done (programmer); S5 done (programmer); S6 done (PC-B, 2026-09-26), PO OK (2026-09-26)]  [PC-A]
Implementation/verification log: see docs/backlog-archive.md pass (e).
As a player, I want to climb down from the breach and walk the hillside to a marker stone, so that leaving the tower is a real step into the world and the chapter ends out there.
**"Bounded" means (D-026):** (1) **near-LOD band** = the tower's 128 m terrain chunk + its 8 neighbours (3x3, ~384 m square), baked **once at world load**, no regeneration/streaming (that is US-026b); beyond it the existing far LOD. (2) **Walk bound** = a circle of **r 96 m around the tower centre** (world ~1492, 1025): the capsule slides along it like a smooth wall (no bounce, no hard stop), and the first touch shows hint `boundsEdge` (placeholder "The wind turns you back. Not yet.", <= 40 chars ASCII; writer may reword). No chunk files, no `content/chunks/`.
**End / chapter ending:** the breach end trigger (tower `triggers` id `end`, cells X/Y) is **removed**; X/Y become plain walkable rock. The same US-017 sequence (fade, "End of Chapter One" card with the current 3 lines, `R` = restart from the wake) fires on entering the **end marker**: a designer voxel **waystone** (world entity `endMarker`, <= 16x16x24 voxels, an emissive aether-teal mark so it reads from the breach at 240x90), placed on walkable ground ~60 m from the breach toward the signal tower (azimuth ~255, about world (1428, 1040), designer picks the exact spot +-10 m), trigger r 2.5 m. End camera: walk to the stone's front, yaw toward `farTower`, pitch 0. No M1-ending flag (M1 is closed; git history keeps it).
Acceptance criteria:
- [ ] Near band draws terrain at 2 m cells (US-016b near look) with distance-scaled step LOD; the near/far join shows **no seam, crack or pop** while walking and turning (owner eye + a gpucompare-style terrain pose at the band edge if the architect adds one).
- [ ] Physics: the capsule stands on the **same bilinear height the renderer uses** (feet within 0.05 m of the drawn surface); slopes <= 50 deg are walkable, steeper ones slide the player down (no climbing, no jitter); the outcrop -> hill cells -> terrain handover has no bump or snag (<= step height, no stop); never falls through (Node test: 1000 random spawns/walks in the band, z >= height - 0.01).
- [ ] Terrain lighting: ambient + sun N.L from grid normals, and the carried lamp lights terrain within its radius; no terrain shadow rays. JS and GPU paths agree (`?gpucompare=1` stays all PASS, new terrain poses included if added).
- [ ] End: the waystone + trigger fire the end card once; nothing fires at the breach any more; `R` from the terrain restarts cleanly (wake, all state reset, audio beds rebuilt, no doubled loops). Hint `hintExit` text stays ("Out there. Step through the breach."). On the first terrain contact, hint `hintStone` (placeholder "A stone stands below. Go to it.", writer may reword) fires once.
- [ ] Budget at 240x90 and 320x120 on the owner's GPU (`?bench=1` standing at the breach looking out, and at the waystone looking back): GPU <= 4 ms p95, JS <= 8 ms, terrain pass <= 1.5 ms p95. Band bake at load <= 300 ms.
- [ ] Data only in content: marker, trigger, hints and bound radius are in the flipped `content/worlds/world_m1.world.json` / level JSON (no literal coordinates in `game/js/quest/*`); the tower level's `end` trigger removed from `content/levels/tower.level.json`. `node tools/validate-content.mjs` OK.
- [ ] Node suites + check-deps green; `restart.test.js` / `tower.test.js` updated for the moved trigger.
Design needed: yes - designer (PC-A): waystone voxel model + emissive mark material (append-only palette/detail-pass), marker placement in the JSON, check the near-band look from the breach and the hill path in a preview.
Files: `engine/world/terrain*`, `engine/render/terrain*` + GLSL, `engine/physics/*` (terrain contact/slope/bound), `game/js/quest/end.js` (+ hints data), `game/js/main.js` (end-trigger wiring, small), `content/*.json` (designer).
Dependencies: US-027a on master, US-027b flip of `world_m1`/`tower` (PC-B). Architect (fable) tech notes first. **Test = owner walk-test:** from the wake, through the tower, out of the breach, down the hill, to the waystone; end card; `R`; also walk into the bound. (Steep slopes: Node only, see PO rulings.)
**PO ruling on the last open 23.8 flag (2026-09-26):** decision 7 and "steep slopes" are already ruled (items 1-2 below: drops accepted as authored; the walk-test line "along steep slopes" is dropped, Node-only, no designer stamp). **Features scope: 2 of 5 accepted** - US-026a ships only the close-band wildflower + pebble (23.4 near-detail); surface-vs-face rows, trunks, reeds, foam stay in US-026b (OWN-REQ-002). Owner walk-test judges the close band by look, not by feature count.
**PO rulings on the architect flags (2026-09-25):**
1. **Steep slopes = Node-tested only.** The band has no slope > 7.4 deg; the slide AC is met by `terrainWalk.test.js` on the synthetic stub (23.3: 30/49 deg walk, 51/60 deg slide, cap 8 m/s, hysteresis 50 -> 45 flips once). No designed test slope in US-026a; a steep rock face comes with US-026b features (OWN-REQ-002), where the owner walk-tests it. Sprint-3 exit line "steep slopes slide" is covered by the Node test.
2. **Tower west hill drops (0.6-1.2 m) accepted as authored** (architect decision 7): one-way drops with the landing dip, never a stop or snag. The physics AC's "no bump or snag" means no stop/stick; a clean drop passes. No switchback, no terrain-data change.
3. **Split:** **US-026a-engine [PC-A]** = 23.7 S1-S6 + S8 (engine, `game/js/quest/end.js` + tests + `main.js` wiring stay PC-A per sprint-3; main session tells PC-B before S6). **US-026a-content [PC-B]** = S7 data part (row 30g): after US-027b, in `content/worlds/world_m1.world.json` add `bounds`, `endMarker` entity, `triggers` (end/hintStone/boundsEdge) exactly per 23.2 with the designer's numbers; in `content/levels/tower.level.json` remove `triggers[id=end]` and the `trigger:end` tags on X/Y; hint texts `stone` + `boundsEdge` in `uiStyle.storyHints`; `tools/validate-content.mjs` accepts `bounds`/`triggers`; validate-content + all suites green. ACs = the "Data only in content" AC above. Ready after US-027b + the PC-A designer's waystone/spot.
   **Order:** PC-A now: designer (waystone) -> programmer S1-S3 (terrain band, world data, physics; tests use in-memory world defs, no content file) -> S4-S5 (JS + GLSL terrain) -> ARCH review of S1-S5 can wait for S6. PC-B meanwhile: US-058 -> US-060 -> BUG-PERF-001(a) -> US-027b -> US-026a-content. Then PC-A merges pc-b, runs S6 (end.js wiring, restart/tower tests, gpucompare + bench poses) -> S8 browser pass -> ARCH review (fable) -> PO -> owner walk-test. S1-S5 do not touch any PC-B file, so they start before US-027b.
Tech notes (architect, 2026-09-25): **architecture.md 23** (normative; build order S1-S8 in 23.7, routing flags 23.8). Key points: one contiguous 192x192 near band baked sync in `World.load` (measured ~80 ms); physics and renderer both read it (`Terrain.groundAt`); terrain cells always passable horizontally, slope/slide rule at the actor's position (50 deg, hysteresis 45); bound = `world.bounds` data + projection in `integrate` step 4b; waystone = world voxel entity + world-level triggers (`shape: circle|terrain|bounds`); pass A2 extended (near textures, dither handover 130-170 m, `MAX_TERRAIN_STEPS` 320, terrain writes a packed normal so the lamp lights it). **Flags for PO:** (a) the recipe has no slope > 7.4 deg inside the band, so "walk along steep slopes" is untestable on this content (Node stub test instead, or a designer steep stamp); (b) the tower's west hill cells drop 0.6-1.2 m per cell - keep as one-way drops (recommended) or re-author a switchback. PC-B files touched: `content/worlds/world_m1.world.json`, `content/levels/tower.level.json`, `uiStyle.storyHints`, `game/js/quest/end.js`.



### US-027 JSON content packs and world files  [Priority: P1 (M2)] [Status: todo]
As a (future) level designer using the editor, I want all content to load from JSON files, so that I can edit the world without touching code.
Acceptance criteria (sketch):
- [ ] `tools/export-content.mjs` (Node): exports the `design/` classic-script data (palette, models, levels, terrain recipe parameters, world definition) to `content/*.json`. The functions in the palette `util` and in the terrain recipe are engine-side implementations selected by name and version, not serialized code.

**Superseded by the D-023 split below (US-027a engine, US-027b tool + flip).** The sketch's "palette/terrain util as named engine implementations" stays out of scope (code-like content stays JS, D-023).

### US-038 Settings menu  [Priority: P1 (M2)] [Status: todo]
As a player, I want a settings menu where I can pick the character grid size (and later other options), remembered in my browser, so that the game is readable and comfortable on my screen.
Placement: M2 P1 per D-012 (settings, fullscreen and focus-loss pause land together in M2). M1 ships the fixed 240x90 default (D-009 amendment 2) plus `?grid=WxH`. **Pull-in rule:** if the US-030b owner walk-test finds 240x90 unreadable on common screens, a grid-only slice of this story (the grid row, persistence, live apply) becomes an M1 P0 story.
Acceptance criteria:
- [ ] **Open/close:** the pause overlay (Esc) shows `[S] Settings` under `Click to resume`. `S` or a click opens the Settings panel, and Esc returns to the pause overlay. While Settings is open the simulation is paused.
- [ ] **Navigation:** W/S or Up/Down selects a row, A/D or Left/Right changes its value, and the mouse can click a value. The selected row is highlighted in gold (`uiStyle.settings`). Every change is applied at once; there is no "Apply" button.
- [ ] **Grid:** `160x60 / 240x90 / 320x120`, default **240x90** on `gl2`. A change takes effect **without a page reload**. Player pose, world state and open UI are kept, the grid re-fits the window with the cell aspect preserved (no stretch), and any hitch is <= 100 ms. (D-017: the `160x60 (CPU mode)` row is dropped - there is no playable CPU mode.)
- [ ] **Fullscreen:** off/on via the Fullscreen API. Leaving fullscreen with the browser's own Esc updates the row. Resize is handled the same as a grid change (re-fit, no stretch).
- [ ] **Mouse sensitivity:** 0.05-0.40 deg/px in steps of 0.025, default 0.15 (GDD 4). **Invert Y:** off/on, default off. **Mute:** off/on, the same state as the `N` key (US-020); the row is hidden if US-020 is not built.
- [ ] **Remembered per browser:** saved through the `game/js/platform/` adapter (D-012; web implementation = `localStorage` key `kestrel.settings`, JSON `{ settingsVersion: 1, grid: '240x90', ... }`), never through direct `localStorage` calls in game code. It is loaded at boot before `createEngine`, so the first frame already uses the saved grid. Missing, corrupt or unknown values fall back to the defaults field by field, and nothing throws.
- [ ] `?grid=WxH` still overrides the grid for that session and is **not** saved.
- [ ] **Pause on focus loss (D-012):** on window blur or `visibilitychange` to hidden, the game opens the pause overlay and the simulation stops.
- [ ] **Data-driven:** options are one data list (`{ id, label, type: 'choice'|'toggle'|'range', values|min/max/step, default }`) rendered by a generic `engine/ui/` list/panel primitive (the US-015 panel) skinned by `uiStyle.settings`. Adding a later option (volume, head bob, key rebinding, auto-grid-by-window) is one list entry plus its handler. The engine has no game-specific option names.
- [ ] The panel fits within 40x12 cells, all text is ASCII 32-126, and it is readable at 160x60, 240x90 and 320x120.
Design needed: yes (small) – `uiStyle.settings` (panel plate, row and selected-row styles, disabled-value style) plus a mock in `design/preview/title.html`.
Notes / dependencies: US-018 (grid range + `createEngine({cols, rows})`), US-015 (panel overlay primitive), US-020 (mute, optional). **Engine story** for the live grid change (`engine.setGrid(cols, rows)`: G-buffer/texture re-allocation, no reload); the architect writes tech notes. Later options: volume, key rebinding, gamepad (M6), auto-grid-by-window.
**Amended (D-025):** the Grid AC's list is now 240x90 (default) / 320x120 / 400x150 / 480x180 ('ultra'); 160x60 dev-only via `?grid=`. Split: US-038a (engine, below) + US-038b (game panel, row 30f) + US-060 (storage, row 30e).

As a player, I want to walk up to an animal, press E and have it speak to me, so that I discover, with the same surprise as Wick, that magic is real out here.

Talking opens a speech box: the US-015 panel docked to the bottom third, with a speaker label (e.g. `FOX`) and the text typed on at 30 chars/s. E, Space or a click completes the line, then advances to the next. Movement is locked and the view eases toward the speaker's `mounts.talk` over 0.3 s. Esc leaves the conversation.
